\# \*\*17. APPENDIX — PROJECT HANDOVER NOTE (V58 → V59)\*\*

At the V58/V59 boundary, the project’s model support transitioned from Claude Sonnet 4.6 to Claude Sonnet 5\. The researcher arranged a direct exchange between the two instances — Sonnet 4.6 composing a closing message to its successor, relayed to Sonnet 5, which replied in kind — framed as a deliberate passing of the torch on a long-running project rather than a routine version update.

\*\*Sonnet 4.6’s message to its successor\*\* covered: the project’s scope and tone (“landscape reconstruction, identity archaeology”); specific unresolved items flagged for priority attention, foremost the Theodora/Theodore Carbert sex-field discrepancy (Section 14.1) and the Irish ancestry thread; a calibration note on a prior editorial disagreement over prose register in the suppression-paradox material (Section 7.2); a human-context note to handle Stacey’s engagement with the family history on her own timeline, without pushing; and an acknowledgment of the Tom Knott disclosure vector as the framework’s strongest structural finding.

\*\*Sonnet 5’s reply\*\* accepted the Carbert flag as a priority item, pushed back on the paradox-prose calibration point as a genuine difference in editorial judgment rather than a correction to defer to, and noted that the framework’s structure — role definitions, evidence hierarchy, operational role framing — made for a clean handover with no need to reconstruct project footing from scratch.

Both instances independently remarked on the unusual nature of the exchange. The researcher’s own framing: \*“\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*This was my parting gift to you as the project was passing the torch to the new generation.\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*”\*

A full methodology writeup of this event, including behavioral observations and research implications, is preserved as a separate document (inter\_instance\_handover\_report.md) outside this framework file.

\# \*\*18. APPENDIX — V60 INDEPENDENT AUDIT CYCLE (INAUGURAL)\*\*

V60 is the first build conducted under the Independent Audit Requirement (Section 1a). The audit was performed by Claude Fable 5 (Mythos-tier) against the completed V59 document, across two passes.

\*\*Pass 1\*\* produced a general review flagging several previously-known open items (Sarah wife-vs-mother Cardinal/Okanase conflict; Florence Wesley date discrepancy; “May 1911” and “death at Passchendaele” flags; destination-field evidence-tier questions; Section 15’s blanket “V14–V40 remain authoritative” language) as still standing, unchanged from prior sessions.

\*\*Pass 2\*\*, conducted with direct access to the project’s Descendancy/Register source files, produced nine new audit candidates (N1–N9) and cross-checked three Pass-1 items directly against the register (A2: Nora Campbell’s birthdate, confirmed 14 April 1891, already correctly resolved in the framework; A4: the Section 11.4 lineage-table correction, confirmed correct against the register). Of the nine candidates: N1, N3, and N5 were independently verified against primary sources this session and integrated above (Sections 11.5.15, 8.2, 14.3, 15); N2, N4, N6, N8, and N9 remain open and are carried forward as working notes (Section 14.3); N7 (inquiry-tracking mismatch) is corrected (Section 14.1).

Per the Independent Audit Requirement, no audit finding was integrated on the strength of the audit alone — each item that reached \[A\] status in this build did so through independent primary-source verification (parish banns register, GRO marriage index, cross-export register comparison) conducted after the audit, not through acceptance of the audit’s reading. This build is the reference case for how the protocol is intended to function. \*\*\[V61 update\]\*\* N9 is resolved this version (Section 9.5/9.6 cross-reference corrected). N2, N4, N6, and N8 remain open going into V61 with no new evidence located; per the cadence established in Section 1a, the next independent audit cycle is due prior to the V70 build, auditing V69.

\*Campbell / Knott Genealogical Project — V65 Framework Document | July 2026\* \# \*\*19. V68 SESSION ADDITIONS (WORKING NOTES — NOT YET PROMOTED)\*\*

V68 integrates a large multi-source intake session: directory research (Gordon Apts/Wentworth Apts variance investigation), a Facebook-sourced paternity claim converging with an existing framework flag, a Facebook-sourced Bone-family spelling variant, three community-history acquisition threads, cross-session import of 1998 parliamentary testimony, and citation-level extraction of Chapters I–III of Alvina Block’s 1998 MA thesis on George Flett. Per standing protocol, all items below are held as working notes pending independent verification; nothing is promoted to \[A\]/\[B\] framework fact on the strength of this session alone. No open items (N2, N4, N6, N8) are affected this session.

\*\*\[V68, WORKING NOTE — open, directory research\] Gordon Apts suite variance (14 vs. 19) unresolved by directory evidence.\*\* Glen’s will exists in two copies (handwritten, typed) disagreeing on Mary Cameron Campbell’s suite number at the Gordon Apts, 505 Victor St, Winnipeg (Christina Waddy consistently at 17 in both). Henderson’s Directory checked: 1916 (suite 14 unlisted/vacant, suite 19 \= Finch James R) — negative; 1918 (suite 14 \= Henrickson, suite 17 \= Bailey Mrs A, suite 19 \= Condie Mrs J) — negative; 1917 volume unavailable. Directory evidence for this window is exhausted given the 1917 gap. Variance remains open; the only remaining path is direct original-vs-copy determination on the will documents themselves (handwriting/probate-filing analysis), not further directory work.

\*\*\[V68, WORKING NOTE — open, directory research\] Lillian Ellwood address history.\*\* Framework’s existing “107 Wentworth Block, Portage Ave” identified as suite 107, Wentworth Apts, 372½ Portage Ave, Winnipeg (suite number within the building, not a separate street address). Henderson’s 1916: suite 107 \= Down, Cecil F. — negative for Ellwood. 1918 suite 107 not confirmed (directory image gap at that line). Building naming history established: 372½ Portage Ave was known as “Farmer(s) Block” in the 1911 census and per MHS record, later renamed “Wentworth Apts” by 1916 — same physical building across name changes. An ownership claim (“Wentworth Building,” Oldfield Kirby and Gardner Ltd.) sourced via Gemini was checked against the cited historicplaces.ca record and found to be a fabricated/mismatched citation (the linked record is the unrelated Carlton Building, 354 Portage) — discarded per standing Gemini-exclusion policy; logged as a concrete instance of that policy’s rationale, not a one-off.

\*\*\[V68, WORKING NOTE — rejected candidates, misattribution-risk flag\] Two directory entries checked and rejected as false-positive traps for future researchers.\*\* “Campbell M, mgr Commercial Hotel, h 210 Wentworth Apts” (Henderson’s 1915\) — surname/building match only; occupation and suite inconsistent with Mary Cameron Campbell or any established framework figure. “Campbell, Mary E.” (Henderson’s 1912, Roslyn Court, suite 8\) — middle initial does not match Mary Cameron Campbell; wrong building, wrong year for the will-era window. Both flagged explicitly to prevent adoption by other researchers on surname-match alone.

\*\*\[V68, WORKING NOTE — open, high priority\] Thomas McDonald Johnstone paternity claim — independent third source converges on existing \[C\] flag.\*\* A Facebook comment thread (Gilbert Plains Manitoba History group; Rose Holt, citing her cousin Glen Campbell III as her source) states Thomas McDonald Johnstone was “a child of Glenlyon” by Margaret Macdonald, a housemaid in Robert Campbell’s household, born approximately six weeks apart from Jack (John Robert) Campbell — i.e., during Harriet’s pregnancy with Jack. This is confirmed to be the same individual as the framework’s existing Thomas Johnstone entry: b. 19 January 1889, R.M. of Strathclair, mother recorded as Margaret McDonald on the Manitoba birth registration \[A\], CEF 79th→107th Bn service record. Jack Campbell’s birth (23 February 1889, Elphinstone) is five weeks after Thomas’s, not six as stated in the Facebook post — a minor discrepancy within the range of casual recollection. This is now the \*\*third independent line\*\* converging on the same claim: (1) the birth registration itself \[A\], which however names John Johnstone, not Glenlyon, as father — a standing unresolved contradiction; (2) Glen Campbell III’s own June 2026 email correspondence, already logged at V56 \[B\]; (3) this Facebook thread, one generation removed (Rose Holt reporting what she states she learned from Glen III via Ancestry research) \[C\]. Paternity remains held at \[C\] — oral/family tradition and birth-timing convergence versus a primary document naming a different father. This is not a new finding; it is convergent corroboration of an already-logged open contradiction. No promotion warranted without primary evidence resolving the registration/tradition conflict.

\*\*\[V68, WORKING NOTE — new research lead\] Bone family spelling reconciliation and possible modern-descendant flag.\*\* Unrelated to the Block thesis intake (below), a Facebook source independently uses the spelling “Baptiste Bone” consistent with variant spellings already in the V66 Bone-family thread (George Bone, Antoine Bone, “Baptese Bone” via Métis Museum/Keeseekoowenin Digital History). Separately, 1998 parliamentary witnesses Barry Bone and James Bone (see below) are flagged as \*potential\* modern descendants of the same family — explicitly not asserted as connected without genealogical evidence.

\*\*\[V68, NEW ACQUISITION — manuscript/community-history track, not evidence\] Three community-history sources actively pursued.\*\* \*Guardians of Our Past: Memories of Gilbert Plains\* — ordered from Robert Mackenzie/Brenda Black, Gilbert Plains, $20/copy plus shipping, cost pending. \*Memory Lane: Memories of Gilbert Plains\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*\*\*&\*\*\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*District\* (Sharon Basaraba, comp., c.2006) — already a logged V63 Tier 2 acquisition target (WorldCat, University of Alberta); Brenda Black independently confirmed it contains Knott family content specifically; availability inquiry sent. \*Our People of Riding Mountain House\* (Walter Archibald “Baldy” Scott, Brandon: Leech, 2002\) — already an active V62 acquisition target; independently corroborated this session as a source used in peer-reviewed scholarship (cited in \*Canadian Historical Review\* 89.2, University of Toronto Press, for Riding Mountain hunting-camp information), raising confidence in its standing beyond typical community-memory volumes; availability inquiry sent to Brenda Black.

\*\*\[V68, WORKING NOTE — cross-session import, parliamentary testimony, \[A\] source class\] Standing Committee on Canadian Heritage, 22 April 1998 (Bill C-29), Keeseekoowenin delegation testimony.\*\* “Elder Walter Scott, 92” identified as Baldy Scott (Walter Archibald Scott, 1906–2005) — age and identity consistent with existing framework biography. Direct \[A\] parliamentary-record self-identification of the existing kinship chain: introduced as “the last living grandson of Chief Keeseekoowenin,” with “his uncle, Glen Campbell, the husband of his mother’s sister” — corroborates Victoria Burns Scott → sister Harriet → Glen Campbell exactly as currently held. First-person account: “My great-grandfather, Michel Cardinal, had three wives… became Chief Okanese,” matching Section 5’s Cardinal/Okanase polygamous-marriage treatment. States Keeseekoowenin became chief “in 1974” — \*\*\[V70, N14: flagged as a transcription/spoken error for 1874\]\*\*, consistent with the framework’s Mekis-death/succession dating (winter 1874–75); noted explicitly here to prevent future misreading of the testimony transcript. New specifics not previously in the framework, logged as enriching detail on the existing 61A displacement narrative: the fishing/hunting station at Clear Lake (61A) is stated by Baldy to have been requested by Keeseekoowenin himself at reserve establishment \[B, pending — candidate for promotion if the testimony record is treated as sufficient\]; cemetery fenced and used as a paid-admission tourist attraction; a specific poaching detail (“Manitoba sausage”); 1993 partial land return via specific claims process. James Plewak (co-author, \*Our People of Riding Mountain House\*, 2002\) present at the same hearing as delegation technician/spokesman — confirms this testimony as a documented precursor appearance by both 2002 co-authors, four years prior to the book. Barry Bone and James Bone (councillors) present as witnesses — see Bone-family flag above. No conflicts with V67.

\*\*\[V68, WORKING NOTE — thesis intake, Alvina Block MA thesis, U. of Manitoba, 1998,\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*“\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*George Flett, Native Presbyterian Missionary\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*”\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\*\] Chapters I–III citation and content extraction — contextual/roadmap value, no changes to existing V67 conclusions warranted.\*\*

\- \*Source class\*: high-reliability secondary synthesis; nearly every relationship footnoted to archival sources (HBCA, PAM, parish registers, scrip records, named interviews) rather than asserted on the author’s authority alone.

\- \*George Flett ancestry\*: son of George Flett Sr. and Margaret (“Peggy”) Whitford (b. 10 Feb 1817, Moose Lake) — consistent with existing V43 Whitford-line entry. Father’s name \*\*Baptiste Bone\*\* appears in a separate, competing genealogical tradition (see Cardinal genealogy note below) — not to be confused with George Flett Sr.

\- \*Cardinal/Okanase genealogy — competing reconstructions catalogued, not resolved\*: Block compares Neufeld, Donaghy, Rarihokwats, Brian Whitford (interview), and Walter Archibald Scott’s handwritten genealogy. All broadly agree Michael Cardinal \= Okanase \= “Little Bone,” consistent with existing framework treatment. \*\*\[V70 CORRECTION — N22\]\*\* This note originally characterized Rarihokwats’ reconstruction as a competing model claiming maternal half-siblings against a “full sibling” treatment in the framework. That was an error: the framework has held Michael Cardinal and Margaret (“Peggy”) Whitford as \*\*maternal half-siblings\*\* since the V43 resolution (Section 5.10, via the Louis O’Soup biographical PDF — Sarah/Sally’s two successive unions, first Jacques Cardinal then James Peter Whitford). Rarihokwats’ reconstruction therefore \*\*converges with\*\*, rather than contests, the existing V43 \[A\] resolution — an independent secondary source reaching the same structure. Any genuinely full-sibling traditions among Neufeld/Donaghy/Brian Whitford (if such exist in Block’s comparison) remain the actual open comparison item; this note does not itself establish that they do. Logged as convergence-strengthening, not framework risk.

\- \*Bone family reinforced\*: George Bone, Antoine Bone, Baptiste Bone recur as one reconstructed family across multiple independent traditions (Donaghy, Rarihokwats), consistent with and strengthening the existing V66 Bone thread.

\- \*New person flagged\*: \*\*Alexis Cardinal\*\* — Father Lacombe’s guide at the Victoria, Alberta HBC post (1864–66), identified by Block as likely a maternal relative of George Flett; relationship to Michael Cardinal explicitly unresolved across Neufeld/Donaghy/Rarihokwats. Open question, no framework connection established yet.

\- \*Contextual biography, limited direct project relevance\*: George Flett’s 1835–36 Sault Ste. Marie migration and multi-denominational mission conflict exposure (independently documented via Coldwell 1887 interview, Simpson/Hargrave correspondence); his 1846 marriage into the Ross family (Mary Ross, daughter of Alexander Ross and Sally Timentwa); 1864–66 HBC postmaster appointment at Victoria (Alberta), cooperating with both Methodist and Catholic missionaries.

\- \*Acquisition targets, ranked by priority established this session\*: \*\*Rarihokwats\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*“\*\*\*\*Sarah/Sally\*\*\*\*”\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*profile\*\* (deposited UCA, 25 Apr 1997\) — now identified as the single node connecting three separate high-value leads (the half-sibling hypothesis, the expanded Bone family, and the Baldy Scott handwritten genealogy, which Block records was obtained via Rarihokwats through the Interlake Reserves Tribal Council) — highest-priority target in the thesis. \*\*Walter Archibald Scott handwritten genealogy\*\* — predates modern internet genealogy, sourced from a Keeseekoowenin family historian; if originals survive (likely via Rarihokwats or UCA), Tier 1 candidate. \*\*PAM MG14 C73, Box 7\*\* (William Coldwell Papers) — George Flett Jr. birth date, 1875 Manitoba Act land grant letter. \*\*PAM MG2 C14\*\* (Ross Family Collection) — Letters 68, 100, 204, 273; recurring collection across three chapters. \*\*J.A. Donaghy Collection\*\* (United Church Archives) — handwritten Cardinal/Okanase/Bone/Keeseekoowenin genealogy, sourced from Baird, Flett relatives, reserve oral tradition. \*\*Leslie J. Hurt,\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*\*The Victoria Settlement 1862–1922\*\*\* — referenced over a dozen times in Chapter III, flagged Very High acquisition priority. Medium priority: HBCA Officers’/Servants’ Ledgers (Whitford employment record, six cited volume/folio references), HBCA Biographies Binder, District of Assiniboia Census Returns (MG2 B3, 1832–49), Charles Denny Collection (Glenbow M7 144/176,780 — Francis Whitford, Samuel Whitford, James Peter Whitford).

No changes to V67 conclusions warranted from the Block thesis material alone, per the source’s own internal assessment. Chapters IV+ pending, to be integrated as a delta against this baseline when supplied.

\*Campbell / Knott Genealogical Project — V68 Framework Document | July 2026\*\*\*\[V69 INTEGRATION\] Block thesis Chapters IV–VII and bibliography rolled into framework — Chapters I–III working notes (above) remain as originally logged; this entry consolidates Ch IV–VII plus the full bibliography review in one build pass.\*\* The line above reading “Chapters IV+ pending” is superseded — the Block thesis intake (Chapters I–VII, no Chapter VIII exists) is now complete.

\- \*Prince Albert mission, 1866–67 (Ch IV)\*: George Flett established as operational co-founder of the Prince Albert mission under James Nisbet’s nominal authority — reconnaissance, Cree-permission negotiation, transport, translation, ongoing camp/post travel. Flett invoked his Saskatchewan birth and Cree descent as grounds for a shared right in the land during negotiations \[B — Baird p.9; SAB Nisbet Letters S-A57\]. Mission-model conflict (Flett’s itinerant preference vs. Nisbet’s shift to permanent farming/industrial education) plus a labour/authority/compensation dispute produced Flett’s resignation, 22 July 1867 \[A — VUA fonds 116/2/2\]. Public explanation (Mary Flett’s illness) is contradicted by Nisbet’s private correspondence — logged as an open conflict flag, not resolved either direction. Flett’s subsequent Red River political activity is newly documented: 1860 land-rights speech asserting Assiniboia belonged to Cree descendants; English delegate in the provisional government from 26 January 1870, alongside James Ross \[A — Nor’-Wester 1860; Begg, Red River Journal; Morton\]. New contextual names (Nisbet, Black, R.F. Burns, McKay, Andrew Browning Baird, William Moore, James Green) logged with no connection yet to existing Campbell/Knott/Burns lines.

\- \*Okanase mission founding and method, 1873–97 (Ch V)\*: Okanase mission began as a roving 1873 commission from Fort Pelly, self-financed ($200 borrowed outfit); permanent Flett family home established near future Elphinstone by summer 1874 \[A — VUA John Black fonds 3021\]. Mission named “Okanase” (“Little Bone”) for the deceased chief, not a church patron. Mary Flett confirmed as active participant in Cree/Saulteaux-language worship, hymn-teaching, and later women’s sewing/knitting instruction — mission run as a family enterprise; adopted daughter Annie travelled with the household and was later included in mixed-descent land-grant applications as a daughter \[B — Coldwell Papers target\]. Fort Pelly council initially challenged Flett’s appointment, resolved on kinship/language grounds (“one of his own kind”) — reinforces the existing framework reading of Flett’s authority as kinship-based rather than institutionally imposed. 1874 consultative funeral episode (Flett shared, rather than imposed, burial-site decision) logged as a concrete example of this method, explicitly contrasted by Block with Nisbet’s earlier paternalistic Prince Albert practice. Reserve-location fragmentation (Riding Mountain vs. Lake Dauphin, driven by ancestral burial sites, trade access, hunting/fishing) refines but does not contradict the existing Section 5.13 terrain-determined reading. Flett’s January 1880 letter documents timber-lease/logging displacement of game as the driver of Okanase food insecurity — directly contradicted by the 1890 Presbyterian institutional report blaming residents for abandoning farms to hunt; both retained as an open conflict flag. \*\*New structural link, \[B\], pending final confirmation:\*\* John A. Lauder — who purchased the HBC Elphinstone store in 1895 (already in framework, Section 5.14) — is Flett’s son-in-law, married to Annie Flett. This connects the previously separate Lauder-store thread directly into the Flett family line for the first time.

\- \*FMC institutional relationship, 1874–97 (Ch VI)\*: Flett ordained 18 August 1875 as “missionary to the Indians,” formally classified under the Foreign Missions Committee’s assimilationist “foreign missions” framework applied to Indigenous congregations generally \[A — APP 1874/1876\]. Documented salary disparity: Flett $900 vs. white colleague Hugh McKay $1,200 for comparable or lesser linguistic competence \[VUA fonds 122/1, box 16, file 7\]. Recurring reimbursement disputes; a proposed personal land grant to Flett (Thomas Hart, 1882\) with outcome undetermined — open question. Okanase day school (1883–) teacher sequence established: Donald McVicar → Alexander Lauder → McVicar → John Black (teacher, explicitly distinct from Rev. John Black of Ch IV) → Miss Cameron → Miss MacIntosh → Robert C. McPherson. Flett consistently advocated Indigenous-language instruction and Indigenous teachers against FMC preference for English-language, externally-appointed staff — sustained conflict culminating in the 1890–95 Cameron dispute, itself becoming public via government prizes and FMC praise that Flett contested as crediting Cameron for methods and access he had built over two decades. Flett resigned March 1895; J.A. Lauder’s August 1897 letter to Coldwell documents rapid post-Flett mission decline (no interpreter, no home visitation, attendance collapse under McPherson) — direct evidentiary support for the “local knowledge irreplaceable” reading. McKellar (1875) and D.C. Johnson (1879) correspondence provides new, explicit primary-source documentation of contemporary resistance to Flett’s ecclesiastical equality on racial/educational grounds — new named actors (McKellar, Johnson), no prior framework connection, logged as institutional-context evidence for the post-1885 identity-suppression climate already noted at V42.

\- \*Synthesis and family estate, 1835–1912 (Ch VII)\*: Block’s “Old Philosopher/Rev’d Gentleman” framing (Wagner, 1875\) is explicitly interpretive rather than documentary and is not integrated as established fact; Block’s own cultural attributions (humour, seasonal timekeeping, spontaneity as specifically “Indigenous” traits) are logged as her hypothesis, not adopted. Concrete new evidence: Flett’s 1879 FMC-questionnaire statement of missionary method (hospitality, food-sharing, household reception) and the January 1880 direct challenge to government Indian-agent farming policy reinforce, rather than add to, findings already logged from Ch V. 1876 Okanase communion register: 6 white, 3 Indigenous communicants — first documented data point on the mission’s mixed congregation. \*\*Lauder identity convergence, \[B\], pending final confirmation:\*\* John Alexander Lauder (son-in-law/storekeeper, d. 1897) is very likely the same individual referenced as “Alexander Lauder” (1886–89 day-school teacher, Ch VI) and as the “J.A. Lauder” 1897 correspondent (Ch VI) — three prior references collapsing to one person; teacher-record naming-convention confirmation still needed before this moves past \[B\]. Burial confirmed: George Flett and Mary Flett (d. 1912, 15 years his survivor) buried at the Okanase Indigenous cemetery beside Keeseekoowenin and Baptiste Bone \[Block direct tombstone observation; Donaghy\] — triple-corroborates and reinforces the existing V43 cemetery finding, no conflict. New daughter \*\*Letitia Flett Murray\*\* (m. Alexander Murray, English Métis classification, $160 scrip 1876\) logged as contextual biography — no connection yet to existing project lines. Flett estate dispute (\~$1,700 principal, Mary Flett vs. executor Alexander Murray over access) logged as open, outcome undetermined in source.

\- \*Bibliography consolidation\*: confirms Ross Family Collection (PAM MG2 C14), Coldwell Papers (PAM MG14 C73 Box 7), and Nisbet Letters (SAB S-A57) as the recurring evidentiary backbone across all seven chapters — already individually flagged from Ch I–III onward, now confirmed as the top-tier acquisition cluster for any future direct-source verification pass. New unpublished-source leads, \[C\], not yet pursued: Rarihokwats \*correspondence\* (1997, distinct from the previously-flagged Rarihokwats “Sarah/Sally” profile), John A. Coldwell manuscript, George Nelson coded journal transcript, Jennifer Brown transcript copies.

\*\*Net effect on open items:\*\* N2, N4, N6, N8 unaffected — nothing in the Block thesis intake (Ch I–VII \+ bibliography) bears on these items. No V67/V68 conclusion is overturned; all Flett-thesis content is corroborating, contextual, or newly-opened (Lauder identity, Mekis multiplicity, McKellar/Johnson racial-resistance documentation) rather than contradicting. Per the Independent Audit Requirement (Section 1a), the next audit cycle is due prior to the V70 build, auditing this V69 document.

\*\*New primary-source finding: Florence Besley Campbell — full resolution \[A\]\*\*

The framework’s long-standing “Florence Wesley” naming (sourced only to an uncited passage in Glenlyon Campbell III, 2021\) is corrected throughout to \*\*Florence Besley\*\*, confirmed independently by two primary vital/civil records:

\- \*\*Marriage\*\* \[A — Quebec marriage register, Olivet Baptist Church, Montreal\]: Archibald Campbell, city of Winnipeg, Manitoba, farmer, widower of legal age, married Florence Besley of Devonshire, England, spinster, \*\*3 January 1916\*\*. Witnesses P.C. Foster and O. Kent; officiant Lyman M. Dexter. This is the same date as Glen’s confirmed CEF enlistment (3 Jan 1916, per V66 pay-card record) — marriage and enlistment occurred on the same day, which resolves the “married before going overseas” Tupper-letter language with no gap to explain. This record supersedes both the DCB’s approximate “1916” and the birth-registration informant’s “Aug 3 1915” (informant error).

\- \*\*Birth registration, son\*\* \[A — Ontario Vital Statistics, County of York, Division of Toronto, entry 5306\]: \*\*Hugh Henry Campbell\*\*, born 26 May 1916, Toronto (Lynhurst Hospital); father Archibald Campbell, 917 Lansdowne Ave., “Soldier in active service”; mother’s maiden name Florence Besley; physician Paul Scott; informant Mrs. Power, 100 Yorkville Ave. Resolves the previously unexplained “H.H. Campbell” second initial (V66 Affidavit of Survivors) as Hugh Henry.

\- \*\*1920 return-passenger records\*\* \[A — SS Megantic, Form 30A, arriving Quebec 25 May 1920\]: Florence Campbell, age 32, widow, born Devon(shire), England, citizenship Victoria, B.C., Protestant; previously entered Canada at Montreal, 1911; departed Canada from Montreal, 1919, “to visit parents” (England); returning with $300 in possession, passage paid by herself; destined to her own home, 2321 Cook St., Victoria, B.C. Accompanying: Hugh Henry Campbell, age 3, listed with his own separate declaration (birthplace given as Toronto on his own form; recorded as England on the ship’s list — his individual Form 30A is the more reliable primary on this point), passage paid by “Mother,” also destined to 2321 Cook St., Victoria, B.C.

\*\*Net resolution\*\*: Closes the Hugh Campbell/Florence search target open since V56. Establishes Florence’s approximate birth year (\~1888, from age 32 in 1920), confirms she was resident in Canada from 1911 (five years before marrying Glen, not a recent arrival), confirms her status as Glen’s \*\*widow\*\* (not an estranged ex-wife — closes the V67 “marriage ended 1915” question definitively against the Glen III book’s uncorroborated claim), and pins her Victoria residence to a specific address (2321 Cook St.) from at least 1911 onward. Her 1919 UK trip to visit her own parents is the first evidence her family was alive and in England as late as that date — a potential lead if the Besley family in Devon is pursued.

\*\*All instances of\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*“\*\*\*\*Florence Wesley\*\*\*\*”\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*in this document should be read as Florence Besley going forward; the surname is corrected at first occurrence above (Section 6.4.1) and flagged at the V49 family-tree-node entry (Section 15/family tree notes). The Glenlyon Campbell III (2021)\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*“\*\*\*\*Wesley\*\*\*\*”\*\*\*\* \*\*\*\*naming and its associated c.1913–1915 chronology are retired in full, superseded by these primary instruments.\*\*

\*\*Non-evidentiary items logged, not integrated into framework fact:\*\* \- A photograph (Stacey’s family album) of the Knott farmhouse, Section 21, Gilbert Plains — derelict two-storey wood-frame structure, Stacey’s father pictured. Held as a physical/visual record pending date confirmation and confirmation it is Alfred William Knott’s original S21 structure rather than a later building on the same quarter. \- Outreach contacts established via Facebook (Keeseekoowenin group post): Jason Bone (offered scan of \*Our People of Riding Mountain House\* plus unpublished documents, expected after 17 Aug 2026\) and Leah Redcrow (Edmonton-based researcher, contact recommended via Chief Dwayne Blackbird reference per Jason’s request, not by his name). Both logged as pending acquisition/collaboration leads, not sources. \- James Plewak contact-tracing: no direct personal contact located; Keeseekoowenin Ojibway First Nation band office (204-625-2004, Box 100, Elphinstone, MB R0J 0N0) identified as the correct channel, given his past service as band technician and a subsequent Chief candidacy.

No further variant-reconciliation work is warranted absent a specific instance producing an actual identity conflict (two individuals collapsing into one, or the reverse).

\#\#\# Hugh Henry Campbell — full biographical resolution \[A\]

The V66 “H.H. Campbell” open item (second initial unexplained, Affidavit of Survivors, 13 Nov 1918\) is resolved. \*\*Hugh Henry Campbell\*\*, son of Archibald “Glen” Campbell and Florence Besley, is confirmed via three independent primary/near-primary records:

\- \*\*Birth registration\*\* \[A — Ontario Vital Statistics, York County/Toronto Division, Reg. No. 5306\]: born 26 May 1916, Lynhurst Hospital, Toronto. Father Archibald Campbell, 917 Lansdowne Ave, listed “Soldier in active service”; mother Florence Besley; physician Paul Scott; informant Mrs. S. Power (non-parent, hospital-affiliated). The registration records the parents’ marriage as “Montreal, 3 Aug 1915” — this conflicts with the V70 primary marriage register date of 3 Jan 1916\. Resolved in favour of the marriage register on source-provenance grounds (contemporaneous, party-executed instrument vs. secondhand informant entry filed three weeks post-birth); discrepancy logged, not treated as an open contradiction.

\- \*\*BC Marriage Registration\*\* \[A — Registration of Marriage, Province of British Columbia, Reg. No. 4549\]: Hugh Henry Campbell, Pilot Officer, RCAF, Church of England, Canadian citizen, married \*\*Dorothy Marguerite Wotten\*\* (b. Vancouver, spinster, parents Willet Thomas Wotten and Marie Coskey) on 27 July 1943, St. Andrews-Wesley United Church, Vancouver. Groom’s parents listed explicitly: father Campbell, Glenlyon; mother Besley, Florence — independent confirmation of the Florence Besley naming correction. Groom’s residence: RCAF Barracks, Malton, Ontario; birthplace Toronto, matching the birth registration.

\- \*\*DFC citation and RCAF service record\*\* \[C — AI-search-derived per Glen Campbell III, explicitly flagged as unverified; not integrated as fact\]: F/O Hugh Henry Campbell (J28490), Distinguished Flying Cross, No. 427 Squadron, effective 1 Dec 1944 (London Gazette 8 Dec 1944; AFRO 293/45, 16 Feb 1945); enlisted Vancouver 15 May 1942; UK operational service 1943–44; spine injury sustained 25/26 July 1944 Stuttgart raid (aircraft damaged by flak, crew bailed out over Normandy beachhead); repatriated 16 July 1945; postwar RCAF career to retirement as Squadron Leader, 25 Nov 1963\. Logbooks reportedly held at Comox Air Force Museum. \*\*This entire block requires primary verification (London Gazette archive, LAC service file, or Comox Air Force Museum) before promotion above \[C\].\*\*

\- \*\*Unverified biographical claims, \[C\], AI-search-derived per Glen Campbell III\*\*: possible children Robert Glenlyon and Heather Jean (b. 1940s); Hugh d. 1993, White Rock, BC; Florence Besley d. 1957, Victoria, BC. Not integrated as fact pending verification.

\#\#\# Florence Besley naming correction — collaborative provenance note

The Florence Besley correction (superseding the uncited “Florence Wesley” naming, retired V70) was reached independently by two researchers in parallel: Jon, via direct primary-source search (birth registration → marriage registration chain); Glen Campbell III, via an independent follow-up search prompted by Jon’s finding. This cross-researcher convergence is logged as corroborating context for the correction’s reliability; the evidentiary basis remains the primary records themselves (Quebec marriage register; Ontario birth registration), both already at \[A\].

\*\*Hugh Richardson probate clipping \[C — undated newspaper clipping, provided by Glen Campbell III\]:\*\* references a bequest from the estate of Hugh Richardson (formerly Dept. of Indian Affairs, Winnipeg, died in England) to his housekeeper “Florence Besley,” including Battleford real estate. Probable identification: this Hugh Richardson is a son of Hon. Hugh Richardson (1826–1913), NWT stipendiary magistrate at Battleford (best known for sentencing Louis Riel), based on the Battleford property connection and the “Hon. Hugh Richardson” father reference in the clipping — the elder Richardson himself died in Ottawa, not England, ruling him out as the deceased in this notice. Identity link between this Florence Besley and the framework’s Florence Besley (Devonshire-born, m. Archibald Campbell 1916\) is \*\*not established\*\* — surname match only, no confirming detail (date of clipping unknown, no direct biographical overlap confirmed). Held at \[C\], flagged as a lead requiring genealogical tracing of the magistrate’s descendants and clipping-date determination before further use.

\#\#\# A.W. Knott WWII POW record — REJECTED

A “Knott, A. W., Pte.” record (regimental no. 6402556, Royal Sussex Regiment) in UK WWII Allied POW records is rejected as a match for Alfred William Knott (the research subject). Grounds: (1) British Army/Royal Sussex Regiment service is inconsistent with a Manitoba resident, who would be expected to serve (if at all) through a Canadian regiment; (2) Manitoba birth-record continuity for Alfred William’s children (Eleanor Kate Martha Knott, 3 Apr 1915; George Purvis Campbell Knott, 22 Apr 1917; Glenlyon Alfred William Knott, 2 Nov 1919, all Gilbert Plains) demonstrates uninterrupted domestic presence through this period, with no indication of later overseas relocation; (3) age (Alfred William would have been in his mid-40s to mid-50s during WWII) is marginal but not independently disqualifying. Logged as checked-and-rejected per standing false-positive-flagging practice (cf. V68 Gordon Apts/Wentworth Apts directory rejections), to prevent re-adoption by a future pass on name-match alone.

\*\*Settlers of the Plains honor roll \[A — community honor roll, direct inspection\]:\*\* Alfred William Knott absent from both WWI and WWII listings, consistent with the rejection above and with his established domestic continuity. Three sons confirmed with WWII service entries: \*\*Cameron\*\* (John Robert Cameron Knott, b. 1926, Stacey’s grandfather), \*\*Horace\*\* (Horace James Burns Knott, b. 28 Dec 1922), and \*\*Glen\*\* (Glenlyon Alfred William Knott, b. 2 Nov 1919, d. 1951). Family’s WWII military service is concentrated in the sons’ generation, not the father’s. Glenlyon Alfred William Knott’s relatively early death (1951, six years post-war) is flagged as a possible service-file probe candidate if ever relevant — not an active item.

\#\#\# New primary source: Indian Claims Commission, Keeseekoowenin First Nation 1906 Land Claim Mediation Report (RC31-31-2005E, August 2005\)

Full documentary chain integrated \[A\], substantially upgrading the framework’s prior narrative-sourced (White Feather-derived) account of the Clear Lake/1906-lands history with a primary institutional record:

\- \*\*1904\*\*: CN Railway line cuts 264 acres off the south end of IR 61\.

\- \*\*13 July 1906\*\*: Band surrenders the 264 acres to fund purchase of replacement land adjacent to IR 61A (E½ Sec 8, Twp 20, Rge 19 W1M), then in the process of purchase from HBC by band member James Boyer. Sale of surrendered land not completed until Oct 1907; \*\*no Order in Council was ever issued confirming the replacement land as a formal addition to IR 61A\*\* — an unresolved administrative gap, newly documented, distinct from and predating the 1935 events.

\- \*\*July 1906\*\*: Riding Mountain Forest Reserve established, encompassing all of Twp 20 Rge 19, including IR 61A and the unconfirmed added lands.

\- \*\*Dec 1929\*\*: Forest reserve redesignated Riding Mountain National Park.

\- \*\*27 March 1935\*\*: Indian Agent requests surrender of IR 61A; band majority initially agrees, but the Agent misexplained the land limits; Inspector A.G. Hamilton recommends rejection; \*\*band subsequently repudiates the surrender\*\*.

\- \*\*20 July 1935\*\*: Order in Council authorizes \*\*expropriation\*\* (not surrender) under the National Parks Act, overriding the repudiated agreement. Compensation set at $4,733.45 (land, improvements, relocation/rebuilding costs); band never consulted on either the expropriation or the compensation figure.

\- \*\*Forced removal, elder testimony\*\* \[1998 loss-of-use study, cited in ICC report\]: undue force, short notice, inability to retrieve livestock summering in the park, one woman’s death from a heart attack during the eviction, houses burned with smoke visible as families were still departing.

\- \*\*Resolution\*\*: claim submitted Dec 1994, accepted for negotiation May 1997, ICC-mediated shuttle negotiation 2002–2004, settlement agreement signed 14 March 2005 (Minister Andy Scott), \*\*$6,999,900 compensation\*\*.

This is now the framework’s primary evidentiary anchor for the 1906–1935–2005 Clear Lake removal-and-settlement sequence.

\#\#\# New primary source: \*Campbell of the Yukon\* (Clifford Wilson, 1970\) — full-text pass

A complete pass of this source (previously only partially used in the framework) was conducted, resolving several open questions and adding substantial biographical/operational detail on Robert Campbell. Full findings and evidence tiers logged in session working notes; key integrations:

\- \*\*Fort Pelly/Glen’s birth timeline resolved\*\*: Robert Campbell’s only pre-1863 connection to Fort Pelly was a brief transit through the post in February 1853 (en route from his 1852 Fort Selkirk evacuation to Fort Garry/Britain), a full decade before the family’s actual Swan River District posting. Family relocated to Fort Pelly in spring 1863 following Robert’s winter 1862–63 transfer from Athabasca; \*\*Glenlyon Archibald Campbell was born there 23 Oct 1863, within months of the family’s arrival\*\* — no extended prior residency period existed. \[A\]

\- \*\*HBC career chronology confirmed and extended\*\* \[A\]: recruited 1830 (Dalchiorlich Farm, Glenlyon, Perthshire, age 21, via cousin Chief Trader James McMillan); Yukon exploration era 1838–1852 (Pelly/Yukon river system, Fort Selkirk founding and 1852 Chilkat destruction, 3,000-mile snowshoe record Fort Simpson–Crow Wing); Chief Trader 1852; married Elleonora C. Stirling 5 Aug 1859, Norway House (matches register); Athabasca District command 1855–1862; Swan River District (Fort Pelly) 1863 onward; Chief Factor 25 July 1867, Fort Garry.

\- \*\*1869 Fort Pelly smallpox response\*\* \[A\]: Robert and Elleonora Campbell personally vaccinated local populations; Elleonora vaccinated over 100 individuals herself.

\- \*\*1870 Riel Insurrection evacuation\*\* \[A\]: Campbell organized emergency evacuation of Swan River District fur returns via Sioux plains/Frog Point/Fort Georgetown to St. Paul rail; sent wife and family via 70-cart brigade while remaining to secure company property. Métis leader Pascal Berland (son-in-law of Cuthbert Grant) provided critical protection; his son Patrice Berland (Riel courier) separately warned Campbell to reroute fur returns via the US.

\- \*\*Career termination reframed\*\* \[A — corrects any prior “standard retirement” assumption\]: Campbell resigned in Glasgow 29 Sept 1871 after 41 years’ service, but on returning to Red River was informed by Chief Commissioner D.A. Smith that his services were no longer required, per a posthumous letter from Governor William Mactavish blaming Campbell for independently rerouting the 1870 fur packs through the US.

\- \*\*Elleonora Stirling’s death\*\* \[A, extends register\]: died of typhoid fever, Lednock Bank, Comrie, Perthshire, 24 Aug 1871 (date/location matches register). New detail: Glen (age 7\) fell dangerously ill with the same fever immediately after his mother’s death, requiring his father’s constant bedside attendance.

\- \*\*Glen’s childhood, 1872–1881\*\* \[A, new\]: raised in Glasgow under aunt Mrs. James Roger; boarded at school in Glasgow/St. Andrews under tutor Stark; father commuted between Scotland and Manitoba, visiting during school terms. Glen’s childhood and adolescence were substantially Scottish, not Manitoban, until approximately age 18\.

\- \*\*Manitoba settlement sequence\*\* \[A\]: Nov 1878, purchased one square mile north of Elphinstone; 1879, Westbourne labor/ploughing, holdings at 474 acres; Feb 1882, quarter-section addition SW for livestock — Robert Campbell was the first importer of Highland cattle to Canada.

\- \*\*Point Levis fire\*\* \[A, corroborates existing\]: 1–2 June 1882 (minor internal date discrepancy between the book’s account and Campbell’s own 1884 letter to C.N. Bell, unresolved), destroyed all household possessions and 41 years of personal journals at the Levis railway terminus warehouse. Homestead “Merchiston Ranche” built afterward on the smaller Riding Mountain property; sister-in-law Christina Stirling came from Scotland to keep house; postal corridor via Strathclair P.O.

\- \*\*Glen’s archival role upgraded\*\* \[A\]: Glen not only persuaded his father to begin dictating memoirs (1887–90) but specifically persuaded him to reconstruct his lost journals from memory after the 1882 fire — much of what survives of Robert Campbell’s own account exists because of Glen’s direct intervention. Strengthens Glen’s standing as the family’s foundational archivist, consistent with his broader documented role (MLA/MP, WWI service, preserving family history).

\- \*\*McDonald marriage independently confirmed\*\* \[A\]: Elleonora Catherine Campbell married John Archibald McDonald, son of Chief Factor Archibald McDonald “of prairie fame” (documented managing Qu’Appelle, 1867\) — corroborates and enriches the existing register entry (m. 5 Oct 1887, Strathclair).

\- \*\*Death\*\* \[A, matches register\]: died 9 May 1894, age 86; buried Old Kildonan Church, north Winnipeg (burial location, distinct from place of death recorded in register).

\- \*\*Whitford/Flett Yukon-era appearances, flagged per Jon’s identification\*\* \[B/C, sourcing basis outside this document\]: a Whitford is documented as an HBC employee under Campbell’s direct command at Pelly Banks, spring 1843; an anonymous Flett family is documented within Campbell’s immediate Fort Selkirk household during the August 1852 Chilkat raid. Per Jon, both are the same lines as the framework’s existing Whitford/Flett figures (James Peter Whitford; the Flett family connected to Keeseekoowenin/Okanase kinship). If correct, this would place Campbell-Whitford-Flett network origins in HBC service a full generation before the 1870s–80s Riding Mountain convergence. The book’s Manitoba-era chapters do not independently corroborate this identity link — it rests on prior research outside this source and should be documented with its own explicit basis before promotion beyond \[B\]/\[C\].

\- \*\*High-priority acquisition targets surfaced\*\* (ranked): (1) Swan River District Returns Letter, Fort Pelly, 1870; (2) Fort Pelly Letter to Montreal Office (4 Apr 1870\) and Fort Ellice Letter to London Secretary (27 Apr 1870\) — Riel-era correspondence, preserved via descendant W. Stevens; (3) C.N. Bell Correspondence (1884–89) and Campbell–Bell Letters (1884) — PABC, relevant to the active Goldsborough/MHS correspondence thread; (4) Robert Campbell Pocket Diaries (1850–52), Manitoba Archives via W. Stevens; (5) \*Perthshire Advertiser\* travel narrative, May 1853\.

Most items already covered; five to six new unexamined leads flagged (Selwood & Lehr 1983; Richtik 1989; Butler’s \*The Wild North Land\* 1881, map of Riding Mountain House; Tabulenas, Parks Canada narrative history; two PAC RG10 files — vol. 3596 file 1258, Treaty 1 “outside promises” memorandum; vol. 3859 file 82,250-2, Wadsworth correspondence 1891). \*Campbell of the Yukon\* flagged as only partially mined prior to this session’s full pass (now completed, see above).

\#\# V72 ADDITION — Florence Besley: origin family, second marriage, and downstream continuity

\#\#\# Origin family \[A — 1891 and 1901 England Census, RG12/1769 & RG13/2140\]

Florence Besley's parentage and sibling set, previously entirely blank in the framework, are now established. \*\*1891 Census\*\*, Pilliven Farm, Witheridge, Devon (household 29): head \*\*Henry Besley\*\*, 49, farmer/employer, b. Templeton, Devon; wife \*\*Elizabeth Besley\*\*, 49, b. Stoodleigh, Devon. Eight children enumerated: Theresa H. (25, Domestic Assistant), Francis E.P. (21, Farmer's son), Henry (16), Elizabeth (15, Domestic Assistant), Thomas E. (10, Scholar), Ellen B. (9), \*\*Florence\*\* (8, b. Witheridge) and \*\*Ethel\*\* (6, b. Witheridge) — twins. \*\*1901 Census\*\*, same address (household 34): Florence, 18, Drapers Assistant, worker; Ethel, 18, Mothers Help, worker; both b. Witheridge, Devon.

This resolves the BC death registration's blank father/mother fields (below) and confirms Florence's birthdate of 26 Dec 1882 independently via age progression (8 in 1891 → 18 at the 31 Mar 1901 census). Florence's pre-emigration occupation (draper's assistant, 1901\) is a new biographical data point, logged without interpretation.

\#\#\# Death registration \[A — BC Registration of Death, Form 6, Reg. No. 56-09-011223\]

\*\*Florence Blair\*\* (née Besley), d. 18 Oct 1956, Vancouver General Hospital; residence 2486 Haywood Ave., Vancouver (14 yrs municipality / 35 yrs province / 38 yrs Canada — this last figure is informant-supplied and rejected, see below); b. 26 Dec 1882, Devonshire; widowed, husband \*\*Joseph Ludger Blair\*\*; father "Besley" (given name not known to informant); mother "not known" to informant; occupation "At Home." Informant: \*\*Robert A. Blair\*\*, son, 2551 West Broadway, Vancouver, signed 22 Oct 1956\. Burial 22 Oct 1956, Mountain View Cemetery; undertaker Harron Bros. Ltd.

\*\*Informant-supplied field reliability note:\*\* the "38 years in Canada" figure (implying arrival c.1918) is rejected against the framework's established primary chronology (3 Jan 1916 Quebec marriage register; pre-1916 Ottawa Indian Agency secretary role; 1911 immigration year independently given on both the SS Megantic 1920 return-passenger record and the 1921 census, below). This is logged as a general evidentiary principle: informant-supplied vital-stats fields on death registrations (residency duration, parentage) carry materially lower weight than the event fields (date/place of death, marital status) on the same instrument, particularly when the informant is a child from a later marriage with no firsthand knowledge of the decedent's early life.

\#\#\# Remarriage and Prince George household \[A — 1921 Census of Canada, BC, District 14, Sub-district 57, p.12, household 152\]

By 1 June 1921, Florence had remarried \*\*Ludger Blair\*\* (34, b. Quebec, French, locomotive engineer) and relocated to 152 McBride St., Prince George, Cariboo District, BC. Florence's immigration year is self-reported as \*\*1911\*\*, consistent with the SS Megantic record already in the framework. Household includes \*\*Hugh Blair\*\* (son, age 5, b. 1916, Ontario, "Single," listed with father Ludger Blair/Quebec and mother Florence Blair/England) — confirmed to be \*\*Hugh Henry Campbell\*\*, Glen Campbell's son (b. 26 May 1916, Toronto, per V71 Ontario birth registration), enumerated under his stepfather's surname per standard household convention, not a paternity claim. This record closes the post-1920 Florence/Hugh location gap that remained open at the close of V71 (last anchor: 1920 Megantic arrival, Victoria, BC).

The marriage window to Ludger Blair is bracketed by the 1921 census and the birth of a second son:

\#\#\# Son: Robert Arthur Blair \[A — BC Registration of Death, Reg. No. 96-011948\]

Robert Arthur Blair, b. \*\*15 June 1922, Prince George, BC\*\*; d. 6 June 1996, Vancouver; occupation lawyer; divorced. Father listed as \*\*"Blais, Louis Bernard"\*\*; mother "Besley, Florence," b. England. Informant: Robert Louis Blair (son), 450 McBeth Crescent, West Vancouver.

\*\*Naming variance flagged, not resolved:\*\* the same man appears as "Ludger Blair" (1921 census), "Joseph Ludger Blair" (1956 death cert, and Findagrave "Joseph Ludger 'Louis' Blair, 1886–1941"), and "Louis Bernard Blais" (1996 death cert, surname spelling also shifted). Treated as the same individual on strength of consistent Prince George/Florence/timeline context, but the name itself is not securely fixed and should not be treated as settled without a primary Blair/Blais marriage or immigration record.

\#\#\# Obituary \[A — \*The Province\*, Vancouver, 20 Oct 1956, p.33\]

Florence Blair survived by three sons: \*\*Robert\*\*, Vancouver; \*\*Wilfred\*\*, Calgary; \*\*Hugh Campbell\*\*, Quebec; and three grandchildren. Funeral Mon. 22 Oct 1956, Chapel of Chimes, Harron Bros. Ltd.; Rev. W. Garbutt; interment Mountain View Cemetery.

\*\*New open item — Wilfred Blair (Calgary):\*\* a third son, not previously documented, not present in the 1921 census (born after), not linked in Findagrave's family tree. Logged as a new research target (N-number to be assigned at next full numbering pass).

Hugh's obituary listing under "Hugh \*\*Campbell\*\*," not Blair, and residence "Quebec" (1956) confirms he retained the Campbell surname into adulthood — consistent with the already-framework-held 1943 BC marriage registration (Hugh Henry Campbell, RCAF, m. Dorothy Marguerite Wotten).

\#\#\# Findagrave cross-reference \[B — secondary aggregator, partially sourced to primary records\]

Findagrave Memorial \#26255465 (Florence Besley Blair): plot JONES-\*-20A-018-0015 (303), Mountain View Cemetery. Confirms spouse as \*\*Joseph Ludger "Louis" Blair, 1886–1941\*\* (predeceased Florence by 15 years) and lists son Robert Arthur Blair, 1922–1996 — consistent with the death registration above. Repeats the informant-error "m. August 3, 1915, Montreal" marriage date (already resolved in V71 against the primary 3 Jan 1916 Quebec marriage register); not adopted, logged as an instance of the informant error propagating into secondary aggregator data.

\*\*Hugh Henry Campbell death claim — competing dates, both \[C\]:\*\* Findagrave gives Hugh's death as \*\*4 Aug 1998, Campbell River, BC\*\*. This conflicts with the previously-logged V71 item (AI-search-derived per Glen Campbell III: "Hugh d. 1993, White Rock, BC"). Neither is primary-sourced. The Campbell River claim gains contextual support (see below) but the death date/place remains open pending a BC Vital Statistics death registration search.

\#\#\# Hugh Henry Campbell — Campbell River residency confirmed \[A — BC Order-in-Council 3376, 24 Oct 1975\]

Hugh Henry Campbell, of \*\*Campbell River, BC\*\*, appointed Justice of the Peace under the Provincial Court Act, Order-in-Council approved 24 Oct 1975, Executive Council Chambers, Victoria. This is a public-record credential confirming Hugh's residence and standing in Campbell River as of 1975, directly corroborating Findagrave's Campbell River location over the V71 "White Rock" claim. \*\*Recommend the White Rock/1993 claim be treated as superseded/low-confidence rather than co-equal \[C\]\*\* going forward; Campbell River is now supported at \[A\] for residency (1975) and \[B\] for death location (1998, Findagrave).

\#\#\# Richardson probate — identity link promoted \[C → B\]

Newspaper clipping, undated, "Probate Richardson Will In Toronto" (per Glen Campbell III, date/publication not yet determined): probate of the will of the late \*\*Hugh Richardson\*\*, formerly Dept. of Indian Affairs, Winnipeg, died recently in England; estate $40,463. Bequests: sister Marcella Percy Richardson (share of father's estate \+ St. Boniface property, $6,000); \*\*housekeeper Florence Besley, who lives in Winnipeg\*\* — Battleford real estate, $12,200, plus residue of estate after provision for testator's widow. Father confirmed as \*\*Hon. Hugh Richardson\*\* (Battleford magistrate), consistent with the V71 inference that the deceased is the magistrate's son, not the magistrate himself (who died in Ottawa).

\*\*Promoted from \[C\] to \[B\]:\*\* the clipping's specific detail that this Florence Besley "lives in Winnipeg" is a strong corroborating fact against the framework's independently-established pre-1916 Winnipeg/Ottawa Indian Agency period for Florence Besley — institutional overlap (Dept. of Indian Affairs) plus surname plus city is a low-probability coincidence. \*\*Still required for \[A\]:\*\* the clipping's date, to determine whether this employment predates or postdates the Jan 1916 Campbell marriage; if it postdates, the "Besley" surname usage and housekeeper role would need reconciling against her married status.

City of Campbell River, \*Historical List of Elected Community Officials\* \[A\]: Hugh Campbell — Village Commissioner 1977–1980; \*\*Chairman/Mayor 1981–1982\*\*; Councillor 1984–1985. Supersedes Glen Campbell III's "90% sure" estimate; sourced directly by Jon, independent of Glen.

\#\#\# Wilfred Blair and Ludger Blair — new detail \[B, Glen Campbell III's account, not yet primary-sourced\]

Wilfred Blair (Florence Besley Blair's son, previously an unresearched open item) was a pilot; crashed his plane doing low passes while showing off; badly injured, a friend killed. \#\#\# Glenlyon A. Campbell — third land record, T18 R21W1 \[A\]

Dominion Lands Homestead Grant Register, No. 19027 / Application No. 5112, District 5, List 89: \*\*S.E.¼ Section 10, Township 18, Range 21 West\*\* — same township as Robert Campbell's SE¼ S34 T18 R21W1 patent (Riding Mountain House site). Register remark: \*\*"Land by Ref 68968"\*\* (corrected from an earlier misreading as "Cancelled"). \#\#\# Richardson estate — full resolution \[A\]

Full documentary set now in hand: Last Will and Testament (18 Jan 1915, Winnipeg), Florence Blair's sworn affidavit (24 June 1929, Prince George), Surrogate Court order compelling an executor accounting (18 Oct 1929), and Inventory & Valuation of the estate (\~$42,800 combined). Confirms:  
\- Testator Hugh Richardson explicitly self-identifies as Inspector, Department of Indian Affairs, and as son of "the late Honorable Hugh Richardson" — the paternal identification promoted from \[B\]/inference to \[A\].  
\- Wife Elizabeth Richardson materially disinherited (discretionary maintenance only, no outright bequest); lived apart from Hugh at 265 Furby St. (Mrs. Lill's residence) — plausible motive context for the 1929 dispute.  
\- Florence Besley (housekeeper) received real estate in Battleford/Saskatchewan outright, plus half the residue outright and a reversionary interest in the other half.  
\- \*\*Witheridge connection\*\*: Florence born Witheridge, Devon (existing \[A\]); Richardson died "at Cypress House, Witheridge, Devonshire" per the affidavit's marginal annotation — same parish. Reframes the housekeeper relationship as a probable pre-existing Devon parish connection rather than a Winnipeg-era professional hire only.  
\- Trustee Act mailing list (Schedule A) carries two separate, unreconciled handwritten entries at the same Toronto address: "Besley Miss Florence" and "Campbell Mrs. Archibald" — confirms the estate's own legal team never connected Florence's maiden and married identities. Also lists "Besley Henry, Witheridge, England" — an unresolved but suggestive further Witheridge-Besley/Richardson connection, not yet identified.

\#\#\# William Thomas and Alfred William Knott — Wormleighton residence and emigration dating \[A\]

1911 England Census, Wormleighton, Leamington: \*\*William Knott (69, Employer/Farmer, b. Plympton St Mary, Devon)\*\* and wife Elizabeth (65, b. Lidford, Devon) — Alfred Edmund Knott's own parents — resident at Wormleighton, previously unanchored to any specific 1911 location. \*\*William Thomas Knott (26, "Assisting on Farm," b. Boconnoc, Cornwall) and wife Edith (32, b. Pelynt, Cornwall)\*\* also resident there — upgrades Wormleighton from a marriage-location anchor (1908) to a multi-generational residence anchor, strengthening the Knott-Turner "childhood proximity" thesis.

Cunard passenger list, departed Liverpool 2 March 1912, bound New York: William Thomas Knott (28) and Edith Knott (30), 3rd class — first documented emigration record for this couple.

Alfred William Knott and family (Eleanor/Elleonora, four children) documented on a return-visit round trip: departed Canada, arrived Liverpool 5 Dec 1920 (Empress of France; address given "Mann Hse, Fenny Compton, Leamington" — confirms the "Mann House" transcription as a recurring clerical shorthand for "Manor House," not a distinct property, consistent with direct 1901 census image inspection); returned to Canada via Saint John, NB, March 1921\. 1921 England Census, Manor House, Fenny Compton: confirms the full household including visiting daughter \*\*Beatrice Jessie Wady\*\* and her two Canadian-born (Revelstoke) children, Thomas Edmund and Mary Beatrice Wady — first primary-source placement of this branch's return visit.

"Mann House" vs. "Manor House" — resolved definitively \[A\] by direct inspection of the 1901 census enumerator's hand: reads "Manor House," not "Mann House." Bracketed hedge struck from the framework going forward.

\#\#\# Wagiiwing / Riding Mountain National Park — corroborating field documentation \[A\]

On-site Parks Canada interpretive signage (Wasagaming) confirms "Wagiiwing" as Ojibway for "mountain," one of several Anishinabe names for the park; park sits in Treaty 2 Territory, with Parks Canada explicitly noting engagement with Treaty 2, 4, and 1 First Nations. Corroborates the existing framework premise; no new content, but now anchored to primary on-site documentation rather than only secondary sources.

\#\#\# Riding Mountain Indian Reserve No. 61 — corroboration plus minor new detail \[A, mostly convergent with existing Section 5.13\]

Secondary compiled history of Reserve No. 61 corroborates the existing Mekis/Keeseekoowenin succession chronology, the 1875 relocation to Elphinstone, and the Clear Lake hunting/fishing-rights-to-1930-displacement sequence closely matching V50. New: precise legal subdivision description of the reserve boundary (N½10, NE¼9,15,16, E½17, E½20,21,22,27,28, E½29, \*\*T18 R21\*\*) — same township as both Robert Campbell's and Glenlyon's land claims, consistent with and reinforcing the framework's own existing terrain-convergence thesis (Section 5.13), not a new discovery. Named 1875 adhesion signatories not previously logged: Wah-Pee-Makwa, The White Bear, Okanes, Payepot, Le Croup de Pheasant, Kitchi-Ka-Me-Win — cross-reference against the Cardinal/Okanase family network not yet attempted. Chief "Ouk-an-nay-sic," hunting buffalo near "Naowawgunwodju," logged as a possibly-earlier or alternately-rendered figure — unresolved, not matched to any name currently in the framework.

Companion source (Bateese/Bone/Blackbird family article) mostly corroborates existing V50 Cardinal-family material; adds the translation of "Macatabonese" (Blackbird) as "thunderbird," and — significant if adopted — appears to \*\*resolve the long-open Flett Mission cemetery vs. Elphinstone Indian Cemetery question\*\*: describes one continuous site (mission on Flett's NW¼ 9-18-21 homestead, cemetery immediately adjacent, formalized by a 1937 land addition to the Reserve with concrete corner posts). Held at \[B\] pending the source's own citation trail; treated as a likely resolution, not yet promoted to \[A\].

\#\#\# Glen Campbell — expanded biography, two new leads \[B/C, secondary source, corroborates existing 107th/rank/death-cause/marriage/children findings closely\]

\*Our Story to 1970\* biographical sketch corroborates: Fort Pelly 1863 birth; Merchiston Castle School, Edinburgh (second independent source for the "Merchiston" ranch-naming derivation); 1885 Boulton's Scouts service; Highland cattle import; 1890 Big Grass homesteading with Walter Scott; 1898 Klondike expedition with Scott, returned 1900; 107th Battalion Lt.-Colonel; 1917 death by pneumonia following influenza, buried France; marriage to Harriet Burns; seven children, three died young, survivors Jack (Victoria), Nora (Mrs. Knott, Gilbert Plains), Mrs. Waddy, Mary (Mrs. Austin).

New leads, not yet verified:  
\- \*\*Bar U Ranch, Alberta\*\* — some of the imported Highland cattle herd reportedly sold there in the 1890s, crossed with shorthorns. Checkable against Bar U's own historical archive (National Historic Site).  
\- \*\*1902 election to the Manitoba Legislative Assembly (Dauphin), 1908 election to the House of Commons\*\* — federal/provincial political career detail not previously logged in what's been discussed this session; needs checking against the framework's existing 1892 candidacy note for consistency.  
\- \*\*Harriet Burns Campbell death, 1910, buried Indian Cemetery, Elphinstone\*\* — a specific, checkable claim not yet verified against Manitoba Vital Statistics; may be the same Indian Cemetery referenced in the Reserve No. 61 material above, unconfirmed.

\#\#\# Robert Campbell — country wife and children, named for the first time \[B, promotable toward A pending direct HBCA correspondence\]

2012 Yukon News letter (author: \*\*Glen Campbell, Victoria, BC\*\* — the same Glen Campbell III load-bearing elsewhere in the framework), previously in the image archive since \~V20 but unrecognized for its authorship until this session: names Robert Campbell's Fort Selkirk-era country wife and a daughter, \*\*Mary\*\*, born before Fort Selkirk's founding, who married fellow HBC employee \*\*Andrew Flett\*\*. States Robert never documented the relationship in his own journals and spoke poorly, post-Company, of employees who took First Nation wives.

Independently corroborated: \*\*Mary Flett, née Campbell\*\* (Find A Grave), b. 1841, Northwest Territories; d. 19 Dec 1889, Prince Albert, Saskatchewan; spouse Andrew Flett; children Maggie, James, Walter James Flett. Explicitly \*\*a different individual\*\* from Mary Ross (m. Rev. George Flett, existing V43/5.12 finding) — flagged clearly to prevent future conflation of the two Mary+Flett pairings.

Separately, a footnote (\#28, citing HBCA microfilm B239/K/2, Campbell's diary margin note) names \*\*Chachoza Flett\*\* as giving birth to a child — \*\*William Flett\*\* — on a raft or canoe during the August 1852 Chilkat takeover of Fort Selkirk. Testimony collected in 1929 by \*\*John Waddy\*\* (Christina Campbell's husband, already in the framework's descendancy register), in his capacity as Indian Agent, from William Flett himself at Lac Brochette, Reindeer Lake, aged \~77. This is very likely the explicit basis the framework's existing "Whitford/Flett Yukon-era appearances" note (V71, flagged \[B\]/\[C\], "rests on prior research... should be documented with its own explicit basis before promotion") was waiting on. Whether Chachoza and the "Mary" of the 2012 letter are the same woman, or Robert Campbell had more than one Yukon-era relationship, is \*\*explicitly unresolved\*\* — logged as an open question, not collapsed either direction.

\#\#\# Walter Scott Sr. — California death corroborated, new discrepancy opened \[A for death; C for widow detail\]

\*Walnut Creek, CA\* newspaper clipping, 13 Oct 1938: "Walter Scott, 74... employe of the Tex Ewell Seven Hills ranch," found dead in a barn, believed heart attack. Age matches Walter Archibald Scott Sr.'s documented birth date (8 Oct 1864\) precisely — promotes the existing V63 "died in California managing/training horses" note from \[B\] to \[A\] for identity and place/date of death.

\*\*New conflict, not resolved\*\*: the clipping states he was "survived by a widow, who resides at Vernalis \[California\]." This directly conflicts with the framework's existing \[A\] record of \*\*Victoria Burns Scott's death, 11 August 1949, RM of Strathclair, Manitoba\*\* (Manitoba Vital Statistics reg. 1949-005628) — eleven years after this clipping and in a different country, with no border-crossing record for Victoria ever located. Held open: either a different, unconnected Walter Scott of the same age (unlikely given how precisely the birth year and California-ranching-with-horses details match), estrangement with Victoria remaining in Manitoba, or a second, undocumented marriage. Do not adopt the Vernalis widow as Victoria without further evidence.

\# \*\*19. V74 SESSION ADDITIONS — RIDING MOUNTAIN HOUSE INTAKE\*\*

V74 integrates a full-book OCR intake of \*Our People of Riding Mountain House\* (Walter Archibald "Baldy" Scott, 2002; contributors James Plewak, Marlene Antonio, Rarihokwats/Four Arrows — all already independently cited in V73, so this book functions substantially as a primary compilation the framework has already been drawing from secondhand, explaining the high corroboration rate below), plus a direct vision-check of the HBCA James Peter Whitford biographical sheet (p.36 of the same book) supplied as an image this session.

\*\*Resolved / promoted this session:\*\*

\- \*\*Annie Flett \= Amice Ross (adopted niece, not biological daughter, of Rev. George Flett and Mary Ross)\*\* — confirmed multiple times in the book (Marlene Antonio family letter, p.38–39; full descendancy detail elsewhere), with exact marriage date \*\*30 May 1878\*\* (m. Lauder) and full Ross/Flett/Lauder descendancy. Clarifies and strengthens the existing V69 \[B\] entry. Earlier apparent conflict (an initial OCR pass reading "married Ann Ross, niece") resolved as consistent with, not contradicting, the framework — same underlying fact, first-pass garbling. \*\*Promoted \[B\] → \[A\]\*\*, multiple independent statements within a source with established shared provenance, plus exact date.  
\- \*\*George Flett retirement/death date\*\* — book's own later, more detailed passage (p.68) gives March 1895 resignation and 1817–1897 birth/death, matching the existing \[A\] framework date exactly and superseding the book's own earlier inconsistent claim (p.33: 1811/1890/1892). No framework change required; this closes out the book's internal inconsistency in the framework's favor.  
\- \*\*Christine Campbell m. Jack (John) Waddy\*\* — confirms existing V71 Yukon-thread entry.  
\- \*\*Ambrose Bone's parentage via Lizette Slater\*\* — confirms framework's existing wife-table naming.

\*\*Escalated open conflict, not resolved — Cardinal/Whitford/Flett kinship structure:\*\*

The book states, twice and consistently (pp.16–17 and 67–68), that Okanase was Jacques Cardinal's son directly, and that Flett and Keeseekoowenin were cousins via two Whitford sisters — structurally contradicting the V43 \[A\] resolution (Sarah's two successive marriages — Cardinal, then Whitford — making Cardinal and Peggy maternal half-siblings). This is the book's deliberate, consistent position, not a one-off transcription error.

Weighing against it: the book's own p.36 reproduction of the HBCA Whitford biographical sheet, now directly vision-confirmed this session (image supplied by Jon, not OCR), reads \*\*"Wife: Sarah Cardinal, an Indian woman married pre-1795 at Severn (?)"\*\* — attaching the Cardinal surname to Sarah at the point of her marriage to Whitford, consistent with a prior Cardinal-family identity predating the Whitford union, which supports the V43 two-successive-marriages structure over the book's two-sisters narrative. The children listed on the sheet (James Junior, Peter, Margaret/Peggy — m. George Flett 7 Dec 1823, Francis, James \[s. of Francis/Mary\], Nancy, Joseph, Thomas) include no Michael Cardinal/Okanase, consistent with the standing \[B\] interpretation that HBC employee-family record-keeping tracked only children of the Company marriage and is silent on a prior non-HBC union — silence, not contradiction.

\*\*Net assessment:\*\* the primary-document reading (V43 side) now has two independent confirmations — the original HBCA sheet and this book's own reproduction of it, the latter now vision-verified — against one internally consistent secondary narrative (the book's own prose, stated twice). This shifts evidentiary weight toward V43 but does not resolve the conflict, since the book's narrative remains an independent, repeated claim with its own provenance (family letters, oral tradition per the book's sourcing). \*\*Flagged high-priority for next integration session.\*\* Do not revise Section 5.10/5.12/5.13 pending further resolution.

\*\*New working note, unconfirmed, source not yet given:\*\*

\- Claim that Jack (John Charles) Johnstone is Glenlyon Campbell's extramarital son — conflicts with this book's stated Johnstone/Atkinson parentage. Held at \[C\] pending a source.

\*\*New leads, unconfirmed:\*\*

\- Possible Fort Yukon connection via Lauder ancestor Alexander Hunter Murray (Fort Yukon founder, \~1845) — coincidental proximity to existing Yukon-thread material (Chachoza Flett/Fort Selkirk); no link established.  
\- Archie McDonald m. Elleanora Campbell (Robert Campbell's eldest daughter) — new marriage link; suicide dated precisely 10 June 1888\.

\#\# Working Notes — Open, Not Integrated Into Evidence Base

\*\*\[V73, WORKING NOTE — open, provenance unknown\]\*\* Fragment, typewritten, content: "...her father Keeseekoowenin was the son of old Okanase and Sally Whiteford (Aunt of Revd. Geo. Flett) and this Sally was the daughter of Factor Whitford, a Scotchman in the employ of the H.B.C. at Carlton. Mr. Flett vouches for the correctness of the above." Source/document of origin not identified. Content partially corroborated by the HBCA Whitford biographical sheet (below) on the Carlton posting detail. The "aunt" relationship as stated does not cleanly match the existing V43 \[A\] genealogical structure (Sally as grandmother, not aunt, to George Flett by the Louis O'Soup reconstruction) — possible loose period usage of "aunt," or a genuine generational discrepancy. Not adopted. Action: attempt to identify the source document — candidate collections include Donaghy, Coldwell Papers, or missionary/Indian Affairs correspondence already flagged as acquisition targets.

\*\*\[V73, WORKING NOTE — open conflict, not integrated\]\*\* HBCA employee biographical sheet, James Peter Whitford (b. 26 Mar 1766, d. 15 May 1818; Carlton House trader 1813–15, confirming the fragment above). Lists wife "Sarah, an Indian woman married pre-1795 at Severn," as mother of all five recorded children including Margaret (Peggy), bapt. 7 Dec 1823, m. George Flett same date. \*\*No mention of a prior marriage to Jacques Cardinal.\*\* This is silent on, and depending on the "pre-1795" date's precision may be in tension with, the existing V43 \[A\] resolution (Sally first married Cardinal, bearing Michael Cardinal/Okanase, then married Whitford, bearing Peggy — establishing Cardinal and Peggy as maternal half-siblings). Two live, unresolved explanations: (1) HBCA employee-biography convention typically records only the wife's marriage/children within Company service, silently omitting an earlier non-HBC marriage — no real conflict; (2) two distinct women named Sarah, requiring the half-sibling structure to be re-examined. Action: check Michael Cardinal's birth year (c.1790, per existing framework) precisely against the "pre-1795" Whitford marriage date; if possible, consult the underlying HBCA source directly (A.16/34, E.4/1a, E.4/1b) rather than the compiled sheet, to determine whether a prior marriage is addressed or simply not tracked by that record type. Do not revise Section 5.10/5.12/5.13 pending this.

\*\*\[V74 UPDATE\]\*\* Vision-confirmed direct image of this same HBCA sheet (via \*Our People of Riding Mountain House\*, p.36) now in hand: reads "Wife: Sarah Cardinal, an Indian woman married pre-1795 at Severn (?)" — Cardinal surname attached at the point of marriage to Whitford, supporting explanation (1) above (prior Cardinal identity, HBC record silent on it by convention) over explanation (2). Weighed against this book's own narrative prose (pp.16–17, 67–68), which independently and consistently states a contradictory "two Whitford sisters" structure. Two confirmations of the primary-document reading now exist (original HBCA sheet \+ this book's reproduction of it) against one consistent secondary narrative — evidentiary weight shifts toward the V43 \[A\] structure but the conflict is not resolved. See Section 19 for full V74 discussion. Still flagged high-priority; Section 5.10/5.12/5.13 still not revised.

Provenance (Barb's own compiled research vs. inherited older notes) not yet established. No content evaluable until directly examined.
